**Harness changes:** Everything listed above in Harness was created this session.

### 2026-09-24 · S4 details, S6 delete, X4 e2e, README, URL state and toasts

Session: [`70daf4c1`](ai/sessions/2026-09-24-70daf4c1-f90d-4c87-83a3-f50089ed21a8.md) · Stories: S4, S6, X1, X3, X4, P1

**Goal:** Check the web screens (list, detail, create, delete) and fill any gaps, run `/verify`, walk the app with the Playwright MCP, then finish the remaining spec stories.

**What the AI did:**

- Audited first. The detail and delete screens the user believed were "mostly added" did not exist: `routes/property.tsx` was a placeholder and the API had no `property`/`deleteProperty` resolvers (SDL only). There was also no Playwright setup, so `pnpm e2e` ran nothing and `verify` passed without e2e.
- Built S4 and S6 with `/graphql-slice` as vertical slices: repository `findById`/`deleteById`, services with a UUID guard (malformed id → `null` / `PropertyNotFoundError`), union mapping in resolvers, a detail view with a weather snapshot section, and a shadcn AlertDialog for delete.
- Added X4 e2e (happy and `success:false` paths) and a README (X3). `pnpm dev` now runs `prisma migrate deploy` so the app starts in 3 commands.
- Follow-up request: list filters and sort kept in the URL (they survive reload; the detail page returns to the same filtered list), toasts on create and delete (Sonner), and labelled coordinates.
- Browser walk via Playwright MCP against the stub: duplicate create, create, filters (`"  HILLS "`, `85A68`, `Fo%`), sort, detail, delete, not-found, 390px width. The AI deleted only its own test rows and left the user's dev data unchanged.
- 7 commits, one per story or concern. Mixed files were split by rebuilding each commit's tree from a snapshot with a script, and each stage was checked with format, lint, typecheck and tests.

**Corrections & surprises:**

- The two e2e "hangs" were real bugs, not slowness. (1) Vite bound only `::1`, so Playwright's IPv4 readiness check waited forever. (2) Playwright couldn't exit because `pnpm exec` (pnpm 12 native) starts children in a new process group that Playwright's teardown doesn't kill. Fix: `exec node_modules/.bin/...` plus a graceful SIGTERM. Piping through `tail` also hid progress, which made the first run look stuck.
- E2e logs showed two Apollo "cache data may be lost" warnings (`Weather` without an id; `Query.properties` shrinking after delete). Fixed with type policies in `apps/web/src/apollo.ts`.
- The reviewer agent returned DONE (13/13 PASS) plus 6 non-blocking findings, and all were fixed. The most useful one: "no Weatherstack request" tests relied on `onUnhandledRequest`, which a swallowed `fetch` would pass. Tests now assert zero recorded requests, confirmed by temporarily injecting a swallowed call. It also found that services other than create-property could import the Weatherstack client without a lint error.
- shadcn CLI prompted to overwrite `button.tsx`; declined. Its Sonner wrapper imported `next-themes` although the app has no theme provider, so the dependency was removed.
- A web test failed once when run as part of the final `verify`. The AI's first diagnosis (a late async toast) was wrong, and its first fix made the test fail every time. Reading Sonner's source showed the store replays active toasts to a new `<Toaster>`; the fix is `toast.dismiss()` in the test `afterEach`. It was folded into the toasts commit (branch not pushed).
- No shadcn skill was installed although the user asked for one; shadcn docs came through context7 instead.

**Prompts worth noting:**

- "Those were mostly added in the previous session, so confirm if there is anything missing" — the audit found this was not the case, so the session built S4 and S6 instead of polishing them.

### Run prisma migrate diff with --from-config-datasource in Prisma v7

Source: https://www.prisma.io/docs/ai/prompts/prisma-7

Execute schema comparison in Prisma v7 using the configuration datasource flag instead of URL flags.

```bash
prisma migrate diff \
  --from-config-datasource \
  --to-schema schema.prisma \
  --script
```

--------------------------------

### Configure shadowDatabaseUrl in prisma.config.ts

Source: https://www.prisma.io/docs/orm/v7/prisma-migrate/understanding-prisma-migrate/shadow-database

Sets the `shadowDatabaseUrl` in `prisma.config.ts` to manually designate a dedicated shadow database for `migrate dev`. Never use identical values for `url` and `shadowDatabaseUrl` as it can delete all database data.

```typescript
import "dotenv/config";
import { defineConfig, env } from "prisma/config";

export default defineConfig({
  schema: "prisma/schema.prisma",
  migrations: {
    path: "prisma/migrations",
  },
  datasource: {
    url: env("DATABASE_URL"),
    shadowDatabaseUrl: env("SHADOW_DATABASE_URL"), // [!code highlight]
  },
});
```

/** Loads the repo-root `.env` if present. Variables already set in the environment win. */
export function loadRootEnv(): void {
  try {
    process.loadEnvFile(new URL('../../../.env', import.meta.url));
  } catch (error) {
    if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== 'ENOENT') throw error;
  }
}
import { z } from 'zod';
import { loadRootEnv } from './load-env.ts';

const Env = z.object({
  DATABASE_URL: z.url(),
  // Free tier is HTTP-only (SPEC open questions), so the base URL is configurable.
  WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL: z.url().default('http://api.weatherstack.com'),
  WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY: z.string().min(1),
  PORT: z.coerce.number().int().positive().default(4000),
});

export type Env = z.infer<typeof Env>;

export function readEnv(source: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): Env {
  // Only the real process env is backed by the repo-root .env; explicit sources (tests) stay pure.
  if (source === process.env) loadRootEnv();
  const parsed = Env.safeParse(source);
  if (!parsed.success) {
    // Report variable names only; values may be secrets (SPEC S5.2).
    const names = parsed.error.issues.map((issue) => issue.path.join('.')).join(', ');
    throw new Error(`Invalid or missing environment variables: ${names}. See .env.example.`);
  }
  return parsed.data;
}

---
name: verify
description: Run the full quality gate for the property app (Postgres up, `pnpm verify` = format, lint, typecheck, all tests, build, e2e) and then smoke-test the running UI in a real browser via the Playwright MCP, reporting each failure with its likely cause. Use this before any commit or PR, after finishing a story, when the user asks "does everything pass?", "is it green?", "check it works", "run the checks", or wants to confirm the app actually runs, even if they don't say "verify".
---

# Verify

The Stop hook already typechecks and unit-tests after every turn. This skill is the **full** gate:
the checks that need Docker, a build, or a browser, plus judgment about what a failure means.
`pnpm verify` is the single source of truth, and CI runs the same script, so don't reimplement its
steps here.

## 1. Database

Run `docker compose ps`. If Docker itself isn't running (connection refused), tell the user to
start OrbStack/Docker Desktop and stop there. Otherwise run `docker compose up -d --wait` so both
`postgres` and `postgres-test` are healthy before tests start. If the api exists, apply migrations
to the dev DB with `pnpm --filter api exec prisma migrate deploy`.

## 2. The gate

Run `pnpm run verify`. It stops at the first failing stage, in this order: format:check → lint →
typecheck → test → build → e2e. For the failing stage:

- **format:check:** run `pnpm run format`; nothing to diagnose.
- **lint:** a `no-restricted-imports` error means a layering violation (see CLAUDE.md). Move the
  code to the right layer; don't disable the rule.
- **typecheck:** first check whether codegen is stale (`pnpm run codegen`, then retry). Stale
  generated types are the most common cause after a schema edit.
- **test:** an MSW `onUnhandledRequest` error means code is calling a URL with no handler, often

**🔧 Write** `~/Projects/covertree-properties/scripts/check-codegen.mjs`

```
// Codegen drift check: regenerate the committed GraphQL types and fail if anything changed.
// Catches a schema.graphql or query-document edit that was committed without `pnpm codegen`.
// (The Prisma client stays gitignored; `pnpm --filter api db:drift` checks schema vs migrations.)
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';

const GENERATED = ['apps/api/src/__generated__/resolvers-types.ts', 'apps/web/src/gql'];

execFileSync('pnpm', ['run', 'codegen'], { stdio: 'inherit' });

const status = execFileSync('git', ['status', '--porcelain', '--', ...GENERATED], {
  encoding: 'utf8',
});
if (status.trim()) {
  console.error(`\nGenerated GraphQL types are out of date:\n${status}`);
  execFileSync('git', ['--no-pager', 'diff', '--stat', '--', ...GENERATED], { stdio: 'inherit' });
  console.error('\nRun `pnpm codegen` and commit the result.');
  process.exit(1);
}
console.info('Generated GraphQL types match the schema and documents.');

```

**🔧 Bash** — Un-ignore GraphQL codegen output, add drift scripts, run check

```bash
python3 - <<'EOF'
import json,re
p='.gitignore'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("**/__generated__/\n**/src/gql/\n","# GraphQL codegen output is committed (CI checks it for drift); the Prisma client is not.\napps/api/src/__generated__/prisma/\n")
open(p,'w').write(s)
for p,k,v,after in [('package.json','codegen:check','node scripts/check-codegen.mjs','codegen'),('apps/api/package.json','db:drift','prisma migrate deploy && prisma migrate diff --from-config-datasource --to-schema prisma/schema.prisma --exit-code','db:reset')]:
    d=json.load(open(p)); sc=d['scripts']; new={}
    for a,b in sc.items():
        new[a]=b
        if a==after: new[k]=v
    d['scripts']=new
    open(p,'w').write(json.dumps(d,indent=2)+'\n')
EOF
git diff --stat; git status --porcelain apps/api/src/__generated__ apps/web/src/gql; pnpm run codegen:check 2>&1 | tail -4

The drift check works both ways: it flags a stale query document and passes once the types are regenerated. Now the Docker files:

**🔧 Write** `~/Projects/covertree-properties/Dockerfile`

```
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# One Dockerfile for the monorepo; docker-compose.yml builds the `api` and `web` targets.

FROM node:22-slim AS base
# openssl: Prisma's schema engine (`migrate deploy`) links against it.
RUN apt-get update \
  && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends openssl ca-certificates \
  && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/* \
  && npm install -g pnpm@12.6.0
WORKDIR /app

# Dependencies are fetched from the lockfile alone, so this layer survives source edits.
FROM base AS deps
COPY pnpm-lock.yaml pnpm-workspace.yaml ./
RUN pnpm fetch
COPY . .
# postinstall runs `prisma generate` (the Prisma client is not committed).
RUN pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile

FROM deps AS api
RUN pnpm --filter api build
WORKDIR /app/apps/api
ENV NODE_ENV=production
EXPOSE 4000
# Dev dependencies stay installed: the Prisma CLI applies migrations on start.
CMD ["sh", "-c", "node_modules/.bin/prisma migrate deploy && exec node dist/server.js"]

FROM deps AS web-build
# Inlined at build time; the browser calls the API directly.
ARG VITE_GRAPHQL_URL=http://localhost:4000/graphql
ENV VITE_GRAPHQL_URL=$VITE_GRAPHQL_URL
RUN pnpm --filter web build

FROM nginx:1.29-alpine AS web
COPY apps/web/nginx.conf /etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf
COPY --from=web-build /app/apps/web/dist /usr/share/nginx/html

```

server {
  listen 80;
  root /usr/share/nginx/html;

  # Client-side routes (/properties/:id) fall back to the SPA shell.
  location / {
    try_files $uri /index.html;
  }

  # Vite fingerprints everything under /assets.
  location /assets/ {
    add_header Cache-Control "public, max-age=31536000, immutable";
  }
}

Now I'll rewrite the README:

**🔧 Write** `~/Projects/covertree-properties/README.md`

````
# Covertree properties

GraphQL API (`apps/api`, GraphQL Yoga + Prisma + Postgres) and React app (`apps/web`, Vite +
Apollo Client + shadcn/ui) for US property records. Creating a property records the current
Weatherstack weather and the location's latitude/longitude with it.

- Contract and acceptance criteria: [docs/SPEC.md](docs/SPEC.md)
- Design decisions: [docs/adr/](docs/adr/)
- How the AI tooling was used: [AI_WORKFLOW.md](AI_WORKFLOW.md)

## Quick start (one command)

Needs Docker only. Put your Weatherstack key in `.env`, then start Postgres, the API and the web
app:

```sh
cp .env.example .env   # set WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY
docker compose up --build
```

Open http://localhost:5173 (API: http://localhost:4000/graphql). The API applies migrations on
start.

No key, or want to save quota? Use the local Weatherstack stub. It always returns the same
Fountain Hills, AZ weather:

```sh
docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.stub.yml up --build
```

## Environment variables

Copy `.env.example` to `.env` at the repo root. Both apps read it; `.env` is never committed.

| Variable                  | Used by   | Default in `.env.example`                                        | Notes                                                                              |
| ------------------------- | --------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY` | API       | `your-access-key`                                                | **Required.** Your Weatherstack key.                                               |
| `WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL`   | API       | `http://api.weatherstack.com`                                    | Keep `http://` on the free plan (see below). `http://localhost:4999` for the stub. |
| `DATABASE_URL`            | API       | `postgresql://[REDACTED]@localhost:5432/covertree`      | Dev database (`postgres` service).                                                 |
| `TEST_DATABASE_URL`       | API tests | `postgresql://[REDACTED]@localhost:5433/covertree_test` | Integration tests and e2e (`postgres-test` service, tmpfs).                        |
… 88 more lines truncated (6763 chars total)
````

- Root scripts already fan out with `pnpm -r --if-present`, so give each app the same script names.
- Add a README "Run it" section once the api serves: `cp .env.example .env`, `docker compose up -d --wait postgres postgres-test`,

start OrbStack/Docker Desktop and stop there. Otherwise run `docker compose up -d --wait postgres postgres-test` (naming the services;
the default set also starts the api/web containers on :4000/:5173) so both
`postgres` and `postgres-test` are healthy before tests start. If the api exists, apply migrations
to the dev DB with `pnpm --filter api exec prisma migrate deploy`.

## Commands

- `docker compose up -d --wait postgres postgres-test` — Postgres only (dev :5432, test :5433).
  Plain `docker compose up` also builds and starts api :4000 and web :5173.
- `pnpm install` · `pnpm dev` — api :4000, web :5173
- `pnpm codegen` — GraphQL types (a hook runs it when you edit a `.graphql` file)
- `pnpm codegen:check` — generated GraphQL types are committed; CI fails on drift
- `pnpm --filter api exec prisma migrate dev --name <change>` — new migration
- `pnpm test` · `pnpm test:unit` · `pnpm e2e`
- `pnpm verify` — format check, lint, typecheck, all tests, build, e2e. Must pass before commit.

$ prettier --check .

</details>

All three were missing pieces and are now in place. Format, lint, typecheck and both drift checks pass locally, but GitHub has never run the new workflow: nothing has been pushed yet.

**Docker Compose**
- **One command:** `docker compose up --build` now starts Postgres, the API (:4000) and the web app (:5173). A single root `Dockerfile` builds both images, and the web app is served by nginx.
- **Missing key:** Compose reads `WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY` from `.env`. If it's missing, the API stops at startup and names the variable.
- **Stub mode:** `docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.stub.yml up --build` runs against the local Weatherstack stub, so no key or quota is needed.
- **Smoke test:** I ran the full stack in stub mode as a separate Compose project on other ports, because your `pnpm dev` API is on :4000. Create and list worked through the API, a deep link to a detail page loaded in the browser with no console errors, and CORS worked. I tore that project down afterwards. Your dev database wasn't touched.
- **Database-only start:** now `docker compose up -d --wait postgres postgres-test`. I updated CLAUDE.md, the `/verify` skill and the scaffold notes, because a plain `up` would now also try to bind :4000/:5173.

**GitHub Actions** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, sharing one setup step)
- **Jobs:** lint (Prettier + ESLint), typecheck, test, and codegen drift.
- **Codegen drift:** the generated GraphQL types are now committed and currently staged. `pnpm codegen:check` regenerates them and fails on any difference. A second step, `pnpm --filter api db:drift`, fails when `schema.prisma` has a change with no migration. I tested both: a stale query and an unmigrated column each made them fail. The Prisma client itself stays gitignored.
- **Extra jobs:** build + e2e, so the split doesn't drop the coverage the old single job had, and a Docker image build so the Dockerfile can't break unnoticed.
- **Unverified on GitHub:** the workflow uses YAML anchors to share the Postgres service definition. GitHub Actions supports them, but we'll only know it works once it runs on GitHub.

**README:** it now has all six sections: one-command start, an env var table, the Weatherstack plan note, an architecture diagram, how to run tests, and the link to `AI_WORKFLOW.md`. The plan note covers the HTTP-only free plan with its key-over-HTTP caveat, the roughly 100 calls a month, which operations use quota, and what running out looks like. The quota figure comes from ADR 0001/0004.

Nothing is committed yet, and neither `pnpm test` nor `pnpm e2e` has run since these changes. When we get there I'd split it into three commits: the list skeleton, the Docker files, then CI + codegen drift + README. Next up is the reviewer pass over S1–S7.

<result>I found two real bugs in `main...HEAD`. The ReportFindings tool isn't available in this session (ToolSearch returned no match), so both findings are below only.

1. **`apps/web/src/features/properties/property-filters.tsx:122` (low): "Clear" does nothing when only the unsaved inputs have values.**
   - The Clear button is enabled when either the applied filter or the typed-but-not-applied filter is non-empty. Clicking it calls `onApply(NO_FILTER)`.
   - The form only resets its inputs (lines 47–51) when the filter in the URL changes.
   - **Scenario:** no filter is applied. The user types `abc` into City, or types `123` into Zip and presses Apply, which shows "Zip code must be exactly 5 digits." Clicking Clear writes the same empty URL, so nothing changes. The typed text and the zip error stay on screen, and the button appears broken.
   - The existing test (`property-list.test.tsx:195`) only clears a filter that was already applied, so it doesn't catch this.
   - **Fix:** in the Clear handler, also call `setDraft(NO_FILTER)` and `setZipError(undefined)`.

2. **`scripts/archive-session.mjs:83-92` (medium): transcripts with leaked secrets stay on disk.**
   - `archive()` writes the "redacted" transcript, the Markdown rendering and the regenerated `README.md` into `ai/sessions/` first. Only afterwards does `findLeaks` check them.
   - When it finds a leak, `main()` just logs `LEAK …` and sets `process.exitCode = 1`. The files are neither deleted nor quarantined.
   - In `--hook` mode the job runs detached and its output only goes to `ai/sessions/.archive.log`, so nobody sees the warning. The leaking `.jsonl`/`.md` files remain in a folder that gets committed, and the README index links to them.
   - **Scenario:** an access key appears in a form the patterns don't catch (such as a `sk_live_…` key shorter than 16 characters), or an email in an unusual format. `LEAKS` flags it, but the file still ends up in the next commit.
   - **Fix:** write to a temporary location and move the files into place only when `findLeaks` returns nothing, or delete the written files when it finds a leak.

I also checked the API side and found nothing I'd report:
- the Weatherstack client's timeout, body-read race, key redaction and parsing;
- the create-property flow (validation, duplicate check before calling Weatherstack, handling a concurrent create of the same address);
- list filtering (wildcard escaping, blank and bad zip handling);
- id checks for get and delete, and the resolver mappings;
- Apollo cache updates after create and delete, and the URL-driven list state;
- the Docker, compose and env loading setup.</result>

All four reviews are done. **The spec review passed with 44/44 and nothing failing**: 42 P0 criteria (S1–S7 and X1–X4) plus the 2 P1 items. All the P0 criteria have a test named after them except X3, which is the README. Nothing below blocks P0. I checked the two most serious findings myself (#1 and #3), and both are real. Numbered so you can pick; the source is in brackets.

### A. Bugs and security (my picks: 1, 3, 4)
1. **The transcript redactor can let the Weatherstack key into git** [security, code-review, confirmed].
   - `scripts/lib/redact.mjs` misses `KEY="[REDACTED]"`, `KEY: …`, JSON `"KEY": "…"` and `%3D` forms. I ran each one through it, and the key stayed in the output.
   - `archive-session.mjs:83-92` writes the files into `ai/sessions/` before running its leak check. A leak only sets an exit code, and in hook mode nobody sees that.
   - Fix: write to a temp folder and move the files into `ai/sessions/` only if the check is clean. Widen the patterns, and also redact the exact values found in `.env`.
2. **Auto-allowed `pnpm exec *` and `docker compose exec *` can print the key** [security]. Either one can dump env vars (e.g. `docker compose exec api node -e "console.log(process.env)"`), which gets around the `.env` read block. With #1, that key could then be committed. This is a change to your permission settings, so I won't make it unless you ask.
3. **Clear does nothing when the typed filter hasn't been applied yet** [code-review, confirmed]. `property-filters.tsx:122` only changes the URL, so typed text and the zip error stay on screen. Fix: also reset the draft and the zip error.
4. **Compose publishes Postgres and the API on all network interfaces**, and Postgres uses the superuser login `covertree/covertree` [security]. Fix: bind the ports to `127.0.0.1:`.
5. **One request can use up the monthly Weatherstack quota** [security]. About 100 aliased `createProperty` calls in a single operation means about 100 upstream calls. Fix: a rule allowing one `createProperty` per operation, or rate limiting.

### B. Hardening (low)
6. **API image** [security, efficiency, reviewer]:
   - It runs as root and ships the whole workspace with dev dependencies, including 6 audit findings in Prisma CLI dependencies (they're not used at runtime).
   - Fix: a slim runtime stage with `USER node`, and add `**/.env` to `.dockerignore`.
7. **CI permissions:** add `permissions: contents: read` and pin actions to commit SHAs.
8. **Production defaults:** turn off GraphiQL and introspection when `NODE_ENV=production`, limit CORS to the web origin, and add nginx security headers and gzip.
9. **Unpinned MCP package:** `.mcp.json` runs `@playwright/mcp@latest`.
10. **Missing test-database guard:** integration tests truncate whatever `TEST_DATABASE_URL` points at, with no `:5433` check (Playwright has one). Share a single `assertTestDatabase` function.

### C. Fixes at the wrong layer [simplify: altitude]
11. **Create should update the list cache** [also efficiency]: evict `properties` in the create mutation's `update`, as delete already does. Then drop `cache-and-network`, which refetches the list on every visit.
12. **`createServices(env, prisma, overrides)`:** the create integration test currently rebuilds the whole service wiring by hand.
13. **Shared integration setup:** one vitest `setupFiles` entry for the table reset and disconnect, plus a helper that builds the app. Today each test file repeats them.
14. **A separate e2e database**, instead of sharing `covertree_test` with the integration tests, which forces "never run both at once" and `Date.now()` workarounds.
15. **Id checks belong in the repository:** it should handle malformed ids, so services stop repeating the UUID check and the `toLowerCase()` call.
16. **Parse stored weather at the repository:** type `weatherData` with the Zod schema and trim descriptions once, when the data is stored. Today the resolver casts it and trims it on every read.
17. **Keep `readEnv` pure:** load `.env` once at the entrypoints instead of the `source === process.env` identity check.
18. **Remount the filter form with a `key`** instead of syncing it with `prevApplied`. This does not fix #3.
19. **Share the stub constants:** move `FAILING_ZIP` and the stub URL into a module with no side effects, so tests can import them instead of copying.

### D. Duplication [simplify: reuse, simplification]
20. **Web app:**
    - one `US_STATES` constant and a `<StateSelect>` component (currently copied 3 times)
    - one zip-code check (5 copies, 3 of them in the web app)
    - a shared `Field` component for both forms
    - a `<LoadError>` alert, a date formatter, and address and weather helpers shared by the list and detail views
    - early returns instead of the nested ternary in the list
    - smaller cleanups: the curried `update(field)(value)`, the one-line `home.tsx` wrapper, the `...NO_FILTER` spread that is overwritten, and `CreatePropertyInput`, which could just be `Address`
21. **Tests:**
    - `listedProperty`, `serveProperties` and `respondWith` moved into `src/test/properties.ts`
    - `app.test.ts` using `executeOperation`
    - `vitest.config.ts` importing the Weatherstack test constants instead of copying them
    - shared `expectedWeather` and `TEST_ADDRESS`

### E. Efficiency [simplify: efficiency]
22. **Dockerfile:** `COPY . .` comes before the install, so any edit reinstalls everything and rebuilds both apps. Split the steps by app.
23. **CI build caching:** add a buildx cache for the docker job and cache Playwright's browser download.
24. **e2e job:** its `pnpm run build` step isn't used by e2e, which runs against dev servers.
25. **Postinstall:** it still runs GraphQL codegen in both apps, which is redundant now that the types are committed (my change today). Keep only `prisma generate`.
26. **Create mutation:** select the detail fields through a shared fragment, so the detail page after create loads from the cache instead of making a second request.
27. **Hooks:**
    - call the tool binaries directly instead of through `pnpm exec`
    - run typecheck and the unit tests in parallel in the Stop hook
    - merge `codegen.mjs` into the format hook
28. **Small ones:** the filter tests seed data once per test instead of once per file, and `redact()` rebuilds its rules on every line it processes.

### F. Docs, test names, config nits
29. **Docs don't match the code (X1):** the SPEC, CLAUDE.md and the README say the Weatherstack client is "injected through the GraphQL context". The code passes it directly to the create service, which is stricter. Reword the docs.
30. **Test gaps** [reviewer]:
    - S7.3 tests are named `S5.5`
    - the S5.8 "sends nothing" test doesn't assert that no request was sent
    - the S5.4 race test doesn't reliably hit the database unique-constraint path
    - the delete toast also appears for `PropertyNotFoundError`
    - a failed refetch leaves the old list on screen with no notice
31. **Enums copied by hand** in services (`WeatherFailureReason`, `'ASC' | 'DESC'`): a new GraphQL enum value gives no compile error there. This is deliberate for the layering.
32. **Config nits:**
    - compose and `vite.config.ts` restate defaults
    - several CI `env` variables may be unused (the agent's inference, not verified)
    - duplicated header logic in `render.mjs`
    - a pointless alias in `global-setup.int.ts`
    - an unneeded spread in `client.test.ts`
33. **Accepted risk:** the key goes over plain HTTP on the free plan (already in the README). Optionally, only accept icon URLs from Weatherstack hosts.

I'd do **1, 3, 4, 25, 29 and 30** now: they're small and either fix a real bug or tidy up after today's changes. **11, 5 and 6** are worth a look after that, and the rest can wait. Tell me which numbers to apply.

export function archive({ transcript, sessionId, outDir, secrets = [] }) {
  const raw = readFileSync(transcript, 'utf8');
  const entries = parseJsonl(raw);
  const meta = summarize(entries);
  const id = sessionId ?? meta.sessionId ?? path.basename(transcript, '.jsonl');
  if (!meta.started) throw new Error(`No timestamps in ${transcript}; cannot date the session.`);
  const base = `${meta.started.slice(0, 10)}-${id}`;
  const subagents = loadSubagents(transcript, id);
  const scrub = (text) => redact(text, { secrets });
  const scrubJsonl = (text) => text.split('\\n').map(scrub).join('\\n');

  const rawDir = path.join(outDir, 'raw');
  const files = new Map([[path.join(rawDir, `${base}.jsonl`), scrubJsonl(raw)]]);

  files.set(path.join(outDir, `${base}.md`), scrub(renderSession({ entries, subagents })));

  const leaksIn = (file, content) =>
    findLeaks(content, { secrets }).map((l) => ({ file: path.relative(REPO, file), ...l }));
  let leaks = [...files].flatMap(([file, content]) => leaksIn(file, content));
  if (leaks.length) return { base, written: [], leaks };

  mkdirSync(rawDir, { recursive: true });
  for (const [file, content] of files) writeFileSync(file, content);
  // The index is built from the session files on disk, so check it after they are in place.
  const readme = path.join(outDir, 'README.md');
  const index = renderIndex(outDir);
  leaks = leaksIn(readme, index);
  if (leaks.length) {
    for (const file of files.keys()) rmSync(file, { force: true });
    return { base, written: [], leaks };

import fixture from './fixtures/weatherstack-current.json' with { type: 'json' };

/** Matches WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL in vitest.config.ts. */
export const WEATHERSTACK_URL = 'http://weatherstack.test/current';
export const TEST_ACCESS_KEY = 'test-key';

/** Real response recorded by `weatherstack:probe "85268, AZ, USA"` (lat/lon are strings). */
export const weatherstackFixture = fixture;

type Body = Record<string, unknown>;

/** Handler that answers with `body` and records every request URL it receives. */
function respond(body: Body | (() => Response | Promise<Response>), requests: URL[] = []) {
  return http.get(WEATHERSTACK_URL, async ({ request }) => {
    requests.push(new URL(request.url));
    return typeof body === 'function' ? body() : HttpResponse.json(body);
  });
}

/** One MSW handler per Weatherstack outcome (SPEC S5.5). */
export const weatherstack = {
  ok: (requests?: URL[], body: Body = fixture) => respond(body, requests),

    respond({ ...fixture, location: { ...fixture.location, ...location } }, requests),
  networkError: (requests?: URL[]) => respond(() => HttpResponse.error(), requests),
  timeout: (requests?: URL[]) =>
    respond(async () => {

39:    calls.push(variables);
/** Serves `rows` for every Properties query and records the variables of each request. */
function serveProperties(rows: Property[], calls: PropertiesQueryVariables[] = []) {
  return graphql.query<PropertiesQuery, PropertiesQueryVariables>('Properties', ({ variables }) => {
    calls.push(variables);
    const ordered = variables.orderBy?.createdAt === 'ASC' ? [...rows].reverse() : rows;
    return HttpResponse.json({ data: { __typename: 'Query', properties: ordered } });
  });
}

/** Streets in display order; the street is the first text in each row. */
const listedStreets = () =>
  within(screen.getByRole('list', { name: 'Properties' }))
    .getAllByRole('link')
    .map((link) => link.querySelector('span')?.textContent);
```

**🔧 Bash** — Commit cache change; read Yoga app setup

```bash
git add apps/web/src/features/properties && git status --short | grep -v "ai/sessions"; git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'
perf(web): create invalidates cached lists instead of the list always refetching

The list used cache-and-network to pick up properties created while it was
unmounted, so every visit (back from a detail page, back/forward) re-queried
the whole list. Create now evicts every cached `properties` result on
success, as delete already evicts its record, and the list uses the default
cache-first policy.

This also removes the case where a failed background refetch left a stale
list on screen with no notice.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 (1M context) <[REDACTED_EMAIL]>
EOF

### Add Validation Rule with useValidationRule

Source: https://github.com/graphql-hive/graphql-yoga/blob/main/packages/envelop/core/docs/use-validation-rule.md

Integrates a depth-limiting validation rule into the Envelop configuration. Requires the @envelop/core and @graphile/depth-limit packages.

```typescript
import { execute, parse, specifiedRules, subscribe, validate } from 'graphql'
import { envelop, useEngine, useValidationRule } from '@envelop/core'
import { depthLimit } from '@graphile/depth-limit'

const getEnveloped = envelop({
  plugins: [
    useEngine({ parse, validate, specifiedRules, execute, subscribe }),
    useValidationRule(
      depthLimit({
        maxDepth: 12,
        maxListDepth: 4,
        maxSelfReferentialDepth: 2
      })
    )
    // ... other plugins ...
  ]
})
```

--------------------------------

export interface GraphQLResponse<TData> {

  errors?: { message: string; extensions?: { code?: string } }[];
}

/** POSTs an operation through Yoga in-process (ADR-0002); returns raw text and parsed body. */
export async function executeOperation<TData>(
  app: ReturnType<typeof createApp>,
  query: string,
  variables: Record<string, unknown> = {},
) {
  const response = await app.fetch('http://api.test/graphql', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' },
    body: JSON.stringify({ query, variables }),
  });
  const text = await response.text();
  return { text, body: JSON.parse(text) as GraphQLResponse<TData> };
}

**🔧 Bash** — Add validation rule, wire into Yoga, add SPEC S5.9 and tests

```bash
mkdir -p apps/api/src/validation && cat > apps/api/src/validation/single-create-property.ts <<'EOF'
import {
  GraphQLError,
  Kind,
  OperationTypeNode,
  type ASTVisitor,
  type SelectionSetNode,
  type ValidationContext,
} from 'graphql';

export const SINGLE_CREATE_MESSAGE = 'Only one createProperty is allowed per request.';

/**
 * SPEC S5.9: each createProperty makes one Weatherstack call, so aliases
 * (`a: createProperty(…) b: createProperty(…)`) would let one request spend the monthly quota.
 * Rejected during validation, before any resolver runs.
 */
export function singleCreateProperty(context: ValidationContext): ASTVisitor {
  return {
    OperationDefinition(node) {
      if (node.operation !== OperationTypeNode.MUTATION) return;
      if (countCreates(node.selectionSet, context, new Set()) > 1) {
        context.reportError(new GraphQLError(SINGLE_CREATE_MESSAGE, { nodes: node }));
      }
    },
  };
}

/** Root-level createProperty fields, including those inside fragments. */
function countCreates(
  selectionSet: SelectionSetNode,
  context: ValidationContext,
  seenFragments: Set<string>,
): number {
  let count = 0;
  for (const selection of selectionSet.selections) {
    if (selection.kind === Kind.FIELD) {
      if (selection.name.value === 'createProperty') count += 1;
    } else if (selection.kind === Kind.INLINE_FRAGMENT) {
      count += countCreates(selection.selectionSet, context, seenFragments);

feat(api): S5.9 at most one createProperty per request

Each createProperty spends one Weatherstack call, so ~100 aliases in a
single mutation could use up the free plan's monthly quota. A validation
rule (aliases, inline and named fragments included) now rejects a second
createProperty before any resolver runs.

Separate requests are not rate-limited; that belongs in front of the API

# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# One Dockerfile for the monorepo; docker-compose.yml builds the `api` and `web` targets.

FROM node:22-slim AS base
# openssl: Prisma's schema engine (`migrate deploy`) links against it.
RUN apt-get update \
  && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends openssl ca-certificates \
  && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/* \
  && npm install -g pnpm@12.6.0
WORKDIR /app

# Dependencies are fetched from the lockfile alone, so this layer survives source edits.
FROM base AS deps
COPY pnpm-lock.yaml pnpm-workspace.yaml ./
RUN pnpm fetch
COPY . .
# postinstall runs `prisma generate` (the Prisma client is not committed).
RUN pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile

FROM deps AS api
RUN pnpm --filter api build
WORKDIR /app/apps/api
ENV NODE_ENV=production
EXPOSE 4000
# Dev dependencies stay installed: the Prisma CLI applies migrations on start.
CMD ["sh", "-c", "node_modules/.bin/prisma migrate deploy && exec node dist/server.js"]

FROM deps AS web-build
# Inlined at build time; the browser calls the API directly.
ARG VITE_GRAPHQL_URL=http://localhost:4000/graphql
ENV VITE_GRAPHQL_URL=$VITE_GRAPHQL_URL
RUN pnpm --filter web build

FROM nginx:1.29-alpine AS web
COPY apps/web/nginx.conf /etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf
COPY --from=web-build /app/apps/web/dist /usr/share/nginx/html
```

build(docker): slim non-root API image with a separate migrate step

dependencies) running as root, because the Prisma CLI applied migrations
on start. Now:

  `prisma migrate deploy` once; the API starts after it succeeds.
- `api` is node:22-slim with `pnpm deploy --prod` dependencies and `dist`
  only, running as `node` (1.78 GB -> 786 MB; the rest is Prisma's peer
  dependency on its CLI).
- .dockerignore excludes .env files in any directory, not just the root.

EOF

All nine items are fixed and committed, one commit each. The final `pnpm verify` passed, the codegen drift check passes, and the only uncommitted files are the session logs.

| # | Commit | What changed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `8499d6a` | The session archiver now checks for leaks before writing and writes nothing if it finds one. It also catches the quoted, YAML, JSON, camelCase and URL-encoded key formats, and redacts the exact secret values from `.env` (plain, URL-encoded and JSON-escaped). I scanned the transcripts already in `ai/sessions/`: none contains your real key, only fake test values. |
| 3 | `6811776` | Clear now resets the typed inputs and the zip error. The new test fails without the fix. |
| 4 | `9766556` | Compose ports only listen on `127.0.0.1`. I recreated your two databases; your dev data and running `pnpm dev` API are fine. |
| 25 | `eb4f4b7` | Install no longer regenerates the GraphQL types; it only generates the Prisma client. |
| 29 | `08dd5d0` | The SPEC, CLAUDE.md, README and the slice skill's eval now say how the Weatherstack client is really injected. |
| 30 | `d07f2f1` | S7.3 has its own tests. The S5.8 test now counts requests. The S5.4 race test always hits the unique-constraint path; with that path disabled, it fails. Deleting a property that was already gone shows a "Property was already deleted" toast instead of "Property deleted". |
| 11 | `95f3ee3` | Creating a property clears all cached lists, and the list no longer refetches on every visit. This also fixes the stale-list case from #30. |
| 5 | `2552491` | New SPEC criterion **S5.9**: a request can contain at most one `createProperty`, whether through aliases or fragments. Extra ones are rejected before anything runs. |
| 6 | `fbd8e43` | The API image is slim, runs as the non-root `node` user and has no source or dev tooling. A one-shot `migrate` Compose service runs migrations before the API starts. `.env` files are excluded in any folder. |

Things you should know:
- **Only per-request:** #5 limits each request, not how many requests someone sends. Real rate limiting would belong in a reverse proxy if the API is ever exposed.
- **Image size:** the API image went from 1.78 GB to 786 MB. Most of what's left comes from Prisma: `@prisma/client` requires the `prisma` CLI, which brings Studio, React and TypeScript along, so the 6 audit findings from the security review are still in the image.
- **List freshness:** since #11, the list only updates on its own after this browser creates or deletes a property. Changes made by someone else show up after a page reload.
- **Not verified on GitHub:** nothing is pushed yet, so the new CI jobs haven't run there.

```
# Backlog

Work that is known but not scheduled. [SPEC.md](SPEC.md) stays the contract for what ships; an
item moves from here into the SPEC (with criteria) when it is picked up.

## Deferred review findings

From the 2026-09-24 full review (reviewer agent, security review, `/code-review`, `/simplify`).
The reviewer reported 44/44 PASS; none of these block P0. Fixed items from that review are in the
git log (`8499d6a`..`fbd8e43`).

### Security and hardening

- [ ] **Auto-allowed commands can read `.env`.** `.claude/settings.json` allows
      `Bash(pnpm exec *)` and `Bash(docker compose exec *)`, either of which can print environment
      variables and bypass the `.env` read deny. The archiver now redacts exact `.env` values, so
      this is defense in depth. Narrow or remove the two entries.
- [ ] **Rate limiting.** S5.9 caps `createProperty` at one per request; separate requests are
      unlimited. Add per-IP limiting in front of the API (reverse proxy) before it is exposed.
- [ ] **CI permissions and pinning.** Add `permissions: contents: read` to `ci.yml`; pin actions
      to commit SHAs.
- [ ] **Production defaults.** Disable GraphiQL and introspection when `NODE_ENV=production`;
      restrict CORS to the web origin; add nginx security headers (CSP, `nosniff`,
      `frame-ancestors`) and `gzip on`.
- [ ] **Pin the Playwright MCP.** `.mcp.json` runs `@playwright/mcp@latest`.
- [ ] **Integration tests lack a test-database guard.** `test/database.ts` truncates whatever
      `TEST_DATABASE_URL` points at; Playwright refuses anything but `:5433`. Share one
      `assertTestDatabase` from `apps/api/test/test-database-url.ts` (also removes the copied
      default URL in `playwright.config.ts`).
- [ ] **Prisma CLI in the API image.** `@prisma/client` peers on `prisma`, so the CLI, Studio,
      React and TypeScript ship in the runtime image (786 MB), including 6 `pnpm audit` findings
      that are not on the request path. Revisit on the next Prisma release.
- [ ] **Icon URLs.** Weatherstack is plain HTTP on the free plan, so a tampered response could
      set `weather_icons` to any URL (`<img src>`, no script execution). Optionally accept only
      known Weatherstack hosts over https.

### Design (fix at the right layer)

- [ ] `createServices(env, prisma, overrides)` so `create-property.int.test.ts` stops hand-wiring
      the service graph.
… 101 more lines truncated (8400 chars total)

**Backlog (`98d1a7f`).** The note is at `docs/BACKLOG.md`, linked from the README and CLAUDE.md, with two sections:

- **Proposed features:** entries stay proposals until picked up; then their criteria go into the SPEC and the feature is built with `/graphql-slice`. The first entry is **F1 · Maps on the list and detail pages**:
  - **List:** one marker per property in the current filtered result, with the map zoomed to fit them. A marker popup links to the detail page, and the link back returns to the same filtered list.
  - **Detail:** a small map centred on the property.
  - **Data:** no API or schema change and no Weatherstack calls. The list query only needs to add `lat`/`long`; the detail query already has them.
  - **Library:** proposed Leaflet with OpenStreetMap tiles, since it needs no key. The tile URL would be configurable through `VITE_MAP_TILE_URL`, because OSM's tile policy doesn't allow heavy use. MapLibre is noted as the alternative.
  - **Draft criteria and test approach:** tests never load real map tiles, and the list stays the main, accessible view.
  - **Open questions:** which tile provider to use beyond local work, how the map should zoom when results include both Puerto Rico and Guam, marker clustering as the list grows, and loading the map code lazily.

## Summary

The full property-records app on top of the tooling baseline: a GraphQL Yoga + Prisma + Postgres API and a React/Apollo web app. `createProperty` records the current Weatherstack weather and the location's lat/long; nothing else calls Weatherstack.

- **Contract:** [docs/SPEC.md](docs/SPEC.md). Stories S1–S7, cross-cutting X1–X4, P1 (URL-kept filters/sort, skeletons, toasts), plus **S5.9** (at most one `createProperty` per request, to protect the Weatherstack quota).
- **Decisions:** [docs/adr/](docs/adr/) — Yoga over Apollo Server, Prisma over Drizzle, creation fails when weather is unavailable.
- **Layering:** resolver → service → repository, lint-enforced; the Weatherstack client is built in `container.ts` and injected into the create-property service only.
- **Run it:** `cp .env.example .env` (set the key) and `docker compose up --build`; or use `docker-compose.stub.yml` for a local Weatherstack stub with no key.
- **How the AI tooling was used:** [AI_WORKFLOW.md](AI_WORKFLOW.md), with redacted session transcripts in `ai/sessions/`.
- **Deferred work and proposed features** (incl. F1 maps): [docs/BACKLOG.md](docs/BACKLOG.md).

## Review before merge

- Spec reviewer agent: **44/44 PASS** (every criterion has code and a test named after its SPEC ID; X3 is the README).
- Security review, `/code-review` and `/simplify` were run on the whole branch. Fixed here: session-archiver redaction (fails closed, exact `.env` values), Clear-button bug, localhost-only compose ports, S5.9 quota guard, slim non-root API image, list cache invalidation, test gaps (S7.3 naming, S5.8 request count, deterministic S5.4 race). The rest is in the backlog.

## CI

Separate jobs: lint, typecheck, test, **codegen drift** (committed GraphQL types + Prisma migrations vs `schema.prisma`), build + e2e (Playwright against a Weatherstack stub), and a Docker image build. Tests never call the real Weatherstack.

## Test plan

- [x] `pnpm verify` locally: format, lint, typecheck, api 129 + web 33 + scripts 13 tests, build, e2e (happy path and weather-failure path)
- [x] Full Docker stack against the stub: migrate → api → web healthy; create works; two aliased creates rejected
- [x] UI walk in a browser: create → list → filter → detail → delete; skeleton at desktop and 390px; no console errors
- [ ] CI green on this PR (first run of the new workflow)

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF

- **Layering:** `eslint.config.mjs` `no-restricted-imports` per layer (resolvers ↛ Prisma/repositories/Weatherstack; services ↛ GraphQL/Prisma, and ↛ Weatherstack except the create-property service; repositories ↛ services/resolvers).
- **No real Weatherstack in tests:** MSW `onUnhandledRequest: 'error'` + a fake key in the Vitest setup; "no request" tests also record outbound requests and assert none. E2E (`apps/web/playwright.config.ts`) starts its own stub, API and web on separate ports against postgres-test.
- **Same gate everywhere:** `pnpm verify` runs locally, in `/verify`, and in [CI](.github/workflows/ci.yml).

### Permissions (`.claude/settings.json`)

- **Allowed without prompting:** specific `pnpm`, `git` (status/diff/log/add/commit/switch/branch) and `docker compose` subcommands.
- **Not allowlisted, so they prompt:** `git push`, `git reset`, `pnpm dlx`, `docker system …`.
- **Denied:** reading `.env` / `.env.local`, directly and via common shell readers (`cat`, `grep`, `source`, `env`). `.env.example` stays readable. The redactor is the backstop if a secret still reaches a transcript.

### Agent

| Agent                                    | Model / tools         | Why it exists                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ---------------------------------------- | --------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| [`reviewer`](.claude/agents/reviewer.md) | opus · Read/Grep/Glob | Adversarial spec-compliance check with a fresh context. It reports each SPEC criterion as PASS / UNTESTED / FAIL with file:line and test evidence. It complements `/code-review`, which finds bugs, by checking the work against the spec. |

**Considered and dropped:** planner (the main session already has plan mode), explorer (the
built-in Explore agent does this), implementer (it would move the core work into side transcripts
and lose conversation context).

## Session archiving and redaction

- **Files:** `ai/sessions/<YYYY-MM-DD>-<session-id>.md` (readable) and `ai/sessions/raw/<date>-<id>[.<agentId>].jsonl` (redacted raw, main + subagents). The date is the UTC date of the first entry.
- **Rendered:** user prompts (including feedback given when rejecting a plan, and answers to questions), assistant replies, tool calls with inputs, and tool outputs truncated to 40 lines / 4 KB. System reminders and metadata are stripped.
- **Redacted in both files:** emails, `access_key=[REDACTED] query params, `*KEY/SECRET/TOKEN/PASSWORD=[REDACTED] values, bearer tokens, API keys, connection-string credentials, account/organization IDs, and absolute home paths. After writing, the files are re-scanned, and any match fails the run and is logged to `ai/sessions/.archive.log`.
- **Tests:** `scripts/__tests__/archive-session.test.mjs` (`pnpm run test:scripts`).

<!-- Appended by /workflow-notes, oldest first. -->

### CLAUDE.md, what the model must know

[CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) (~50 lines): commands, the resolver → service → repository layering rule,
Weatherstack injection, codegen workflow, test isolation, and when to use each skill and agent.
It's kept short on purpose, and every rule in it that can be enforced is enforced below.

### Hooks (`.claude/settings.json` → `.claude/hooks/`, `scripts/`)

| Hook                                          | Does                                                                                                              | Why a hook, not a rule                                                                                                                        |
| --------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| PostToolUse `Edit\|Write` → `format-lint.mjs` | prettier + `eslint --fix` on the edited file; remaining errors go back to Claude (exit 2)                         | Formatting and lint would otherwise depend on the model remembering; this way layering violations are caught on the edit that introduces them |
| PostToolUse `Edit\|Write` → `codegen.mjs`     | `.graphql` → `pnpm codegen`; `schema.prisma` → `prisma generate`                                                  | Stale generated types are the most common SDL-first failure, so a "remember to run codegen" line isn't enough                                 |
| Stop → `stop-check.mjs`                       | typecheck + unit tests, only if source files changed since the last green run; honours `stop_hook_active`         | The model can't declare "done" on red types. Skipping no-op turns keeps Q&A instant, and there's no infinite loop                             |
| SessionEnd → `archive-session.mjs --hook`     | re-launches detached, then archives + redacts transcript and subagent transcripts, renders MD, rebuilds the index | Archiving has to happen for every session, including ones that end abruptly. SessionEnd hooks get a very short time budget, hence the detach  |


- "Those were mostly added in the previous session, so confirm if there is anything missing" — the audit found this was not the case, so the session built S4 and S6 instead of polishing them.
- "like the filter sort persisting between reloads" — led to an e2e check that reloads a real browser, not only unit tests of the URL helpers.

**Harness changes:** ESLint now restricts Weatherstack imports to the create-property service; Playwright config and e2e stub added (`apps/web/e2e/`); the Enforcement section above was updated to match. No skill, hook, agent or CLAUDE.md changes.

I found two real bugs in `main...HEAD`. The ReportFindings tool isn't available in this session (ToolSearch returned no match), so both findings are below only.

1. **`apps/web/src/features/properties/property-filters.tsx:122` (low): "Clear" does nothing when only the unsaved inputs have values.**
   - The Clear button is enabled when either the applied filter or the typed-but-not-applied filter is non-empty. Clicking it calls `onApply(NO_FILTER)`.
   - The form only resets its inputs (lines 47–51) when the filter in the URL changes.
   - **Scenario:** no filter is applied. The user types `abc` into City, or types `123` into Zip and presses Apply, which shows "Zip code must be exactly 5 digits." Clicking Clear writes the same empty URL, so nothing changes. The typed text and the zip error stay on screen, and the button appears broken.
   - The existing test (`property-list.test.tsx:195`) only clears a filter that was already applied, so it doesn't catch this.
   - **Fix:** in the Clear handler, also call `setDraft(NO_FILTER)` and `setZipError(undefined)`.

2. **`scripts/archive-session.mjs:83-92` (medium): transcripts with leaked secrets stay on disk.**
   - `archive()` writes the "redacted" transcript, the Markdown rendering and the regenerated `README.md` into `ai/sessions/` first. Only afterwards does `findLeaks` check them.
   - When it finds a leak, `main()` just logs `LEAK …` and sets `process.exitCode = 1`. The files are neither deleted nor quarantined.
   - In `--hook` mode the job runs detached and its output only goes to `ai/sessions/.archive.log`, so nobody sees the warning. The leaking `.jsonl`/`.md` files remain in a folder that gets committed, and the README index links to them.
   - **Scenario:** an access key appears in a form the patterns don't catch (such as a `sk_live_…` key shorter than 16 characters), or an email in an unusual format. `LEAKS` flags it, but the file still ends up in the next commit.
   - **Fix:** write to a temporary location and move the files into place only when `findLeaks` returns nothing, or delete the written files when it finds a leak.

I also checked the API side and found nothing I'd report:
- the Weatherstack client's timeout, body-read race, key redaction and parsing;
- the create-property flow (validation, duplicate check before calling Weatherstack, handling a concurrent create of the same address);
- list filtering (wildcard escaping, blank and bad zip handling);
- id checks for get and delete, and the resolver mappings;
- Apollo cache updates after create and delete, and the URL-driven list state;
- the Docker, compose and env loading setup.

</details>

<details><summary>🧩 Explore — Simplify: reuse angle (<code>a282c61285c31505a</code>)</summary>

#### 🧑 User

Code-quality review (NOT bug hunting), REUSE angle, of the repo at ~/Projects/covertree-properties. READ-ONLY: do not edit anything.

Scope: everything on chore/ai-harness vs main — get it with `git diff main...HEAD -- apps scripts Dockerfile docker-compose.yml docker-compose.stub.yml .github .claude/hooks eslint.config.mjs ':!apps/api/src/__generated__' ':!apps/web/src/gql' ':!apps/web/src/components/ui'` (~100 files; main has no app code so this is the whole app). Skip generated files, shadcn ui primitives (apps/web/src/components/ui), ai/sessions, lockfile.

Stack: GraphQL Yoga API (apps/api/src: resolvers → services → repositories/Prisma; weatherstack client; Zod validation), React/Apollo web (apps/web/src/features/properties, routes), Vitest + MSW tests (apps/api/test, *.test.tsx), Playwright e2e (apps/web/e2e), Claude Code hook scripts (.claude/hooks/*.mjs) and session archiving (scripts/).

#!/usr/bin/env node
/**
 * Archive a Claude Code session into ai/sessions/.
 *
 *   --hook                     SessionEnd hook mode: read hook JSON from stdin, re-launch
 *                              detached (SessionEnd hooks get a very short time budget), exit 0.
 *   --transcript <path>        Archive this transcript (manual use / migration).
 *   [--session-id <id>]        Override the id (default: from the transcript).
 *   [--out <dir>]              Output dir (default: <repo>/ai/sessions).
 *
 * Writes (all redacted):
 *   <out>/raw/<date>-<id>.jsonl            main transcript
 *   <out>/raw/<date>-<id>.<agentId>.jsonl  one per subagent
 *   <out>/<date>-<id>.md                   readable rendering
 *   <out>/README.md                        regenerated index
 * Exits non-zero if anything sensitive survives redaction.
 */

async function readStdin() {
  let data = '';
  for await (const chunk of process.stdin) data += chunk;
  return data;
}

async function hookMode(outDir) {
  const input = JSON.parse((await readStdin()) || '{}');

import js from '@eslint/js';
import globals from 'globals';
import tseslint from 'typescript-eslint';

/**
 * Architecture rules (see CLAUDE.md "Layering"): resolver -> service -> repository.
 * The Weatherstack client is built once in src/container.ts and injected into the create-property
 * service; the GraphQL context only carries services, so resolvers can't reach the client.
 */
const layer = (files, patterns) => ({
  files,
  rules: { 'no-restricted-imports': ['error', { patterns }] },
});

// A raw fetch would bypass the import rules (e.g. calling Weatherstack from a resolver), so only
// the Weatherstack client may use it.
const onlyWeatherstackFetches = {
  files: ['apps/api/src/**/*.ts'],
  ignores: ['apps/api/src/weatherstack/client.ts', '**/*.test.ts'],
  rules: {
    'no-restricted-globals': [
      'error',
      {
        name: 'fetch',
        message: 'Outbound HTTP belongs in an injected client (src/weatherstack/).',
      },
    ],
  },
};

I've sent the reuse review back to the main agent; nothing was edited. It lists 14 places where code copies something the repo already has, split into 10 that are more worth fixing and 4 minor ones.

**Web tests**
1. The list-row factory and the fake `Properties` backend are written separately in `property-list.test.tsx`, `property-filters.test.tsx` and `delete-property.test.tsx`. They belong in `src/test/properties.ts`, which already has `detailedProperty` and `serveProperty`.
2. The fake `CreateProperty` backend is written twice, in `create-property-form.test.tsx` and `property-list.test.tsx`.

**Web app**
3. The list of US states is rebuilt from the generated `USState` enum in 3 files, and the state dropdown is written twice.
4. The 5-digit zip rule and its error message are copied in 3 places, even though `validate-property.ts` already exists for this.
5. `property-filters.tsx` redoes the label/error/accessibility wiring that the `Field` component in `create-property-form.tsx` already does.
6. The list and detail views each have their own copy of the error-with-Retry alert, the date format, the address line and the weather summary.

**API tests**
7. `create-property.int.test.ts` builds all four services by hand instead of using `createServices` from `container.ts`. That means it never tests the real wiring.
8. `test/app.test.ts` sends its own POST request instead of using `executeOperation`.
9. The test Weatherstack URL and key are typed out as literals in several files, even though `test/weatherstack.ts` exports them. Two response builders in `client.test.ts` also belong in that file.
10. The expected weather object is repeated in 3 integration tests. The standard test address is repeated in 4 places.

**Minor**
11. The zip rule is duplicated between the create and list services.
12. The Playwright config copies the test-database default URL and its safety check from the api test setup.
13. Two hook scripts repeat the same "which file was edited" logic. `archive-session.mjs` re-implements `readInput` from `.claude/hooks/lib.mjs`.
14. The two Postgres services in `docker-compose.yml` repeat the same settings. `ci.yml` already shares them with a YAML anchor.

**Checked and left out:**
- **API types written by hand in the services:** the generated types would replace them, but the layering rules deliberately keep services free of GraphQL types. The compiler catches any drift.
- **Web validation copying the server's rules:** the spec asks for this.
- **The e2e test redeclaring the failing zip code:** importing it from the stub would start the stub's server.

The shared API test setup, the repository and resolvers, the scripts and CI are clean.

</details>

<details><summary>🧩 general-purpose — Security review of whole repo (<code>a4a7bc57381c55202</code>)</summary>

#### 🧑 User

Do a security review of the whole repository at ~/Projects/covertree-properties. The branch chore/ai-harness vs main is effectively the entire application (main has no app code): `git diff --name-only main...HEAD` lists the files. READ-ONLY: do not modify any file, do not commit, do not run the app against real services. Never read `.env` (it is denied and contains secrets); `.env.example` is fine.

App: GraphQL Yoga API (apps/api: resolvers → services → repositories/Prisma, Postgres) + React/Apollo web (apps/web). createProperty calls the external Weatherstack API (key in env, free plan is HTTP-only so the key goes in a query string over plain HTTP). Also in scope: Dockerfile, .dockerignore, docker-compose*.yml, apps/web/nginx.conf, .github/workflows/ci.yml + .github/actions, scripts/, .claude/hooks/*.mjs and .claude/settings.json (Claude Code hooks that run shell commands on edits), and the session-archiving script scripts/archive-session.mjs + scripts/lib/redact.mjs (it archives AI transcripts into ai/sessions/ — check that redaction actually prevents secrets from being committed).

Focus on real, exploitable issues with high confidence: injection (SQL via Prisma raw queries, command injection in hooks/scripts), secret exposure (logs, error messages, GraphQL errors, client bundle, Docker image layers, CI logs, archived transcripts), GraphQL-specific risks (introspection/error masking in production, query depth/cost, batching), CORS, input validation gaps, SSRF via the Weatherstack base URL/query, container config (running as root, secrets baked into images), CI (pull_request_target, untrusted input in run:, action pinning), dependency risks. Skip theoretical or style-only issues; explicitly say which common concerns you checked and found fine.

Output: a list of findings, most severe first, each with: severity (High/Medium/Low), file:line, the concrete exploit or failure scenario, and a suggested fix. Then a short "checked and fine" list. Keep it concise.

{
  "message": "## Security review: covertree-properties (chore/ai-harness vs main)\n\nThis was a read-only review. I ran the redactor on sample strings in memory and ran `pnpm audit --prod`, and I did not read `.env`. I also scanned the committed and untracked transcripts under `ai/`. They contain no real key: every `access_key=[REDACTED] and `KEY=[REDACTED] is `[REDACTED]`, and the only other key-like strings are fake test keys, a UUID used in a test, a pnpm cache hash and a public npm signing key.\n\n### Findings (most severe first)\n\n**1. High: the transcript redactor misses common secret formats and writes the files even when its leak check fails.** (`scripts/lib/redact.mjs:7,16,62`, `scripts/archive-session.mjs:83-92,156`)\n- **Formats it misses.** `VALUE` excludes quotes and backslashes, so a value has to start with a plain character right after `=`. I confirmed that each of these passes through unredacted and that `findLeaks` also reports nothing:\n  - `WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY=\"[REDACTED]\"`\n  - the JSONL-escaped form `KEY=\\\"[REDACTED]\\\"`\n  - YAML style `WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY: abc…`\n  - JSON style `\"WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY\": \"abc…\"`\n  - Node inspect style `accessKey: '[REDACTED]'`\n  - URL-encoded `access_key%3D[REDACTED]\n- **How it leaks in practice.** `.claude/settings.json` auto-allows `Bash(docker compose exec *)` and `Bash(pnpm exec *)`. These get around the `.env` deny rules. For example, `docker compose exec api node -e \"console.log(process.env)\"` prints `WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY: '…'`, or `pnpm exec node -e …` can read `.env`. The key then goes into the transcript, the SessionEnd hook archives it unredacted into `ai/sessions/raw/*.jsonl` and `.md`, and `git add *` is also auto-allowed, so it gets committed.\n- **The check does not stop anything.** `archive()` writes every file first and only then runs `findLeaks`. A detected leak just sets exit code 1. In `--hook` mode the process is detached and its output goes to the gitignored `.archive.log`, so nobody sees it and the leaking files stay in `ai/sessions/`.\n- **Fix:**\n  - Make the checker fail closed: write to a temp directory, run `findLeaks`, and move the files into `ai/sessions/` only when the check is clean. Otherwise delete them and log the problem.\n  - The archiver runs as a plain node script, so it can load the real `.env` itself. Replace the exact values of every variable there (plus their URL-encoded forms) with `[REDACTED]`.\n  - Widen the rules to cover `KEY: v`, `KEY=\"[REDACTED]\"`, `KEY='[REDACTED]'`, JSON `\"…key…\": \"v\"` and `%3D`.\n  - Add a CI or pre-commit step that runs `findLeaks` over `ai/`.\n  - Remove `pnpm exec *` and `docker compose exec *` from the auto-allow list, or narrow them.\n\n**2. Medium: one request can use up the whole Weatherstack quota.** (`apps/api/src/app.ts:10-13`, `apps/api/src/schema.graphql:213`)\n- There is no auth (a SPEC non-goal), no rate limit and no limit on aliases or root fields. A single POST with about 100 aliased mutations (`a1: createProperty(input:{street:\"1 X\"…}) a2: …`), each with a different street, makes about 100 real Weatherstack calls. That is the free tier's monthly allowance.\n- After that, every create fails with `UPSTREAM_ERROR` until the next month. The duplicate check doesn't help, because it only stops identical addresses.\n- **Fix:**\n  - Allow at most one `createProperty` per operation (a validation rule that counts mutation root fields, or a cost limit such as `@escape.tech/graphql-armor` max-aliases/cost).\n  - Add per-IP rate limiting on the mutation, for example Yoga's `useRateLimiter` or limiting at a reverse proxy.\n  - Optionally add a global daily budget counter.\n\n**3. Medium: Postgres and the API listen on every network interface with a superuser password in the repo.** (`docker-compose.yml:8-9,24-25,44-45`)\n- The ports are published as `'5432:5432'`, `'5433:5432'` and `'4000:4000'`, which bind to 0.0.0.0. Anyone on the same network (for example café Wi-Fi) can log in as `covertree`/`covertree`.\n- That accou

1	# SPEC — Covertree property records
2	
3	Source: Covertree "AI Software Engineer" assessment brief plus product decisions recorded
4	below. This spec is the contract the `reviewer` agent checks against; story IDs (`S1`…`S7`)
5	and criterion IDs (`S3.2`) are stable — reference them in commits, tests and reviews.
6	
7	## Problem statement
8	
9	Covertree needs to manage records of US residential properties through a GraphQL API with a
10	React UI. Each property must be enriched with the current weather and coordinates of its
11	location at the moment it is created, so records carry location data the user never has to
12	enter by hand. A property without that data is incomplete, so it must never be stored.
13	
14	## Goals
15	
16	1. Every user story S1–S7 is implemented end to end (API + UI) and covered by automated tests
17	   named after the criteria below.
18	2. Weatherstack is called **only** inside the `createProperty` mutation — never on reads,
19	   deletes, or in tests.
20	3. No property is ever persisted without Weatherstack weather, `lat` and `long`; every failure
21	   mode reaches the client as a **typed** error in the schema.
22	4. The GraphQL schema is the single contract: server resolvers and client documents are typed
23	   from it by codegen, with zero hand-written duplicate types.
24	5. A reviewer can run the whole app from the README in ≤ 3 commands (plus `.env`).
25	
26	## Non-goals
27	
28	- **Authentication / multi-tenancy** — not in the brief; every user sees all properties.
29	- **Updating a property** — the brief lists create/read/delete only; weather is a creation
30	  snapshot, so edits would make it misleading.
31	- **Refreshing weather after creation** — the brief forbids calling Weatherstack outside creation.
32	- **Address autocomplete / geocoding services** — lat/long come from the Weatherstack response.
33	- **Pagination** — P2; the dataset in scope is small. The list API takes input objects so it
34	  can be added without breaking the contract.
35	- **Non-US addresses** — the brief assumes all properties are in the United States.
36	
37	## Decisions
38	
39	| Topic                  | Decision                                                                                                                                                              |
40	| ---------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

1	import { z } from 'zod';

3	/** Weatherstack sends coordinates as strings (SPEC S5.5); accept numbers too, reject non-numeric. */
4	const coordinate = (min: number, max: number) =>
5	  z
6	    .union([z.string().regex(/^-?\d+(\.\d+)?$/), z.number()])
7	    .transform(Number)
8	    .pipe(z.number().min(min).max(max));

1	import type { PropertyRepository } from '../repositories/property.repository.ts';
2	import { isPropertyId } from './property-id.ts';
3	
4	export type DeletePropertyResult =
5	  { kind: 'deleted'; id: string } | { kind: 'notFound'; id: string };
6	
7	export type DeleteProperty = (id: string) => Promise<DeletePropertyResult>;
8	
9	/** S6 — never touches Weatherstack (S6.5). Unknown or malformed id → notFound (S6.2). */
10	export function deletePropertyService({
11	  properties,
12	}: {
13	  properties: Pick<PropertyRepository, 'deleteById'>;
14	}): DeleteProperty {
15	  return async (id) =>
16	    isPropertyId(id) && (await properties.deleteById(id))
17	      ? // Postgres accepts any case but stores lowercase; return the id as `Property.id` has it.
18	        { kind: 'deleted', id: id.toLowerCase() }
19	      : { kind: 'notFound', id };
20	}

1	import { http, HttpResponse } from 'msw';
2	import { setupServer } from 'msw/node';
3	
4	/** Shared MSW server. Tests add Weatherstack handlers with `server.use(...)`. */
5	export const server = setupServer();
6	
7	/**
8	 * Records every outbound HTTP request and fails it. `onUnhandledRequest: 'error'` alone only makes
9	 * a stray request error, which code could swallow; asserting on this list proves none was made.
10	 */
11	export function recordOutboundRequests(): URL[] {
12	  const requests: URL[] = [];
13	  server.use(
14	    http.all('*', ({ request }) => {
15	      requests.push(new URL(request.url));
16	      return HttpResponse.error();
17	    }),
18	  );
19	  return requests;
20	}
21

1	import { z } from 'zod';
2	import { loadRootEnv } from './load-env.ts';
3	
4	const Env = z.object({
5	  DATABASE_URL: z.url(),
6	  // Free tier is HTTP-only (SPEC open questions), so the base URL is configurable.
7	  WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL: z.url().default('http://api.weatherstack.com'),
8	  WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY: z.string().min(1),
9	  PORT: z.coerce.number().int().positive().default(4000),
10	});
11	
12	export type Env = z.infer<typeof Env>;
13	
14	export function readEnv(source: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): Env {
15	  // Only the real process env is backed by the repo-root .env; explicit sources (tests) stay pure.
16	  if (source === process.env) loadRootEnv();
17	  const parsed = Env.safeParse(source);
18	  if (!parsed.success) {
19	    // Report variable names only; values may be secrets (SPEC S5.2).
20	    const names = parsed.error.issues.map((issue) => issue.path.join('.')).join(', ');
21	    throw new Error(`Invalid or missing environment variables: ${names}. See .env.example.`);
22	  }
23	  return parsed.data;
24	}
25
```

````
1	# Covertree properties
2	
3	GraphQL API (`apps/api`, GraphQL Yoga + Prisma + Postgres) and React app (`apps/web`, Vite +
4	Apollo Client + shadcn/ui) for US property records. Creating a property records the current
5	Weatherstack weather and the location's latitude/longitude with it.
6	
7	- Contract and acceptance criteria: [docs/SPEC.md](docs/SPEC.md)
8	- Design decisions: [docs/adr/](docs/adr/)
9	- How the AI tooling was used: [AI_WORKFLOW.md](AI_WORKFLOW.md)
10	
11	## Quick start (one command)
12	
13	Needs Docker only. Put your Weatherstack key in `.env`, then start Postgres, the API and the web
14	app:
15	
16	```sh
17	cp .env.example .env   # set WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY
18	docker compose up --build
19	```
20	
21	Open http://localhost:5173 (API: http://localhost:4000/graphql). The API applies migrations on
22	start.
23	
24	No key, or want to save quota? Use the local Weatherstack stub. It always returns the same
25	Fountain Hills, AZ weather:
26	
27	```sh
28	docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.stub.yml up --build
29	```
30	
31	## Environment variables
32	
33	Copy `.env.example` to `.env` at the repo root. Both apps read it; `.env` is never committed.
34	
35	| Variable                  | Used by   | Default in `.env.example`                                        | Notes                                                                              |
36	| ------------------------- | --------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
37	| `WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY` | API       | `your-access-key`                                                | **Required.** Your Weatherstack key.                                               |
38	| `WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL`   | API       | `http://api.weatherstack.com`                                    | Keep `http://` on the free plan (see below). `http://localhost:4999` for the stub. |
39	| `DATABASE_URL`            | API       | `postgresql://[REDACTED]@localhost:5432/covertree`      | Dev database (`postgres` service).                                                 |
40	| `TEST_DATABASE_URL`       | API tests | `postgresql://[REDACTED]@localhost:5433/covertree_test` | Integration tests and e2e (`postgres-test` service, tmpfs).                        |
… 88 more lines truncated (7166 chars total)

**🔧 Read** `~/Projects/covertree-properties/scripts/check-codegen.mjs`

<details><summary>Read result (19 lines)</summary>

```
1	// Codegen drift check: regenerate the committed GraphQL types and fail if anything changed.
2	// Catches a schema.graphql or query-document edit that was committed without `pnpm codegen`.
3	// (The Prisma client stays gitignored; `pnpm --filter api db:drift` checks schema vs migrations.)
4	import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
5	
6	const GENERATED = ['apps/api/src/__generated__/resolvers-types.ts', 'apps/web/src/gql'];
7	
8	execFileSync('pnpm', ['run', 'codegen'], { stdio: 'inherit' });
9	
10	// Compare the working tree with the index, so staged-but-uncommitted codegen output counts as current.
11	const git = (...args) => execFileSync('git', [...args, '--', ...GENERATED], { encoding: 'utf8' });
12	const status = git('diff', '--name-status') + git('ls-files', '--others', '--exclude-standard');
13	if (status.trim()) {
14	  console.error(`\nGenerated GraphQL types are out of date:\n${status}`);
15	  console.error('\nRun `pnpm codegen` and commit the result.');
16	  process.exit(1);
17	}
18	console.info('Generated GraphQL types match the schema and documents.');
19
```

1	# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
2	# One Dockerfile for the monorepo; docker-compose.yml builds the `api` and `web` targets.
3	
4	FROM node:22-slim AS base
5	# openssl: Prisma's schema engine (`migrate deploy`) links against it.
6	RUN apt-get update \
7	  && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends openssl ca-certificates \
8	  && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/* \
9	  && npm install -g pnpm@12.6.0
10	WORKDIR /app
11	
12	# Dependencies are fetched from the lockfile alone, so this layer survives source edits.
13	FROM base AS deps
14	COPY pnpm-lock.yaml pnpm-workspace.yaml ./
15	RUN pnpm fetch
16	COPY . .
17	# postinstall runs `prisma generate` (the Prisma client is not committed).
18	RUN pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile
19	
20	FROM deps AS api
21	RUN pnpm --filter api build
22	WORKDIR /app/apps/api
23	ENV NODE_ENV=production
24	EXPOSE 4000
25	# Dev dependencies stay installed: the Prisma CLI applies migrations on start.
26	CMD ["sh", "-c", "node_modules/.bin/prisma migrate deploy && exec node dist/server.js"]
27	
28	FROM deps AS web-build
29	# Inlined at build time; the browser calls the API directly.
30	ARG VITE_GRAPHQL_URL=http://localhost:4000/graphql
31	ENV VITE_GRAPHQL_URL=$VITE_GRAPHQL_URL
32	RUN pnpm --filter web build
33	
34	FROM nginx:1.29-alpine AS web
35	COPY apps/web/nginx.conf /etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf
36	COPY --from=web-build /app/apps/web/dist /usr/share/nginx/html
37
```

1	// Local stand-in for Weatherstack so the running app never spends real API calls.
2	//   node e2e/weatherstack-stub.mjs            → serves the recorded Fountain Hills response
3	//   STUB_MODE=success-false node e2e/…       → HTTP 200 { success: false } (UPSTREAM_ERROR)
4	// In the default mode, zip code FAILING_ZIP also gets { success: false }, so e2e can exercise the
5	// failure path (SPEC X4) against the same running stack.
6	// Point the API at it with WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL=http://localhost:4999.
7	import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
8	import { createServer } from 'node:http';
9	
10	export const FAILING_ZIP = '00000';
11	
12	const port = Number(process.env.WEATHERSTACK_STUB_PORT ?? 4999);
13	const mode = process.env.STUB_MODE ?? 'ok';
14	const fixture = readFileSync(
15	  new URL('../../api/test/fixtures/weatherstack-current.json', import.meta.url),
16	  'utf8',
17	);
18	const failure = JSON.stringify({
19	  success: false,
20	  error: { code: 104, type: 'usage_limit_reached', info: 'Stubbed failure.' },
21	});
22	
23	createServer((request, response) => {
24	  const url = new URL(request.url ?? '/', 'http://stub');
25	  if (url.pathname !== '/current') {
26	    response.writeHead(404).end();
27	    return;
28	  }
29	  const fails =
30	    mode === 'success-false' || url.searchParams.get('query')?.startsWith(`${FAILING_ZIP},`);
31	  response.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'application/json' });
32	  response.end(fails ? failure : fixture);
33	}).listen(port, () => console.info(`Weatherstack stub (${mode}) on http://localhost:${port}`));
34
```
