1	import { SortDirection, USState } from '@/gql/graphql';
2	import { NO_FILTER, type FilterValues } from './property-filters';
3	
4	/**
5	 * The list's filters and sort live in the URL (SPEC P1), e.g. `/?city=hills&state=AZ&sort=oldest`,
6	 * so they survive a reload and can be shared. Defaults are left out to keep URLs short.
7	 */
8	export interface ListParams {
9	  filter: FilterValues;
10	  direction: SortDirection;
11	}
12	
13	const STATES = new Set<string>(Object.values(USState));
14	
15	/**
16	 * Hand-edited or stale URLs must not break the page: an invalid zip or state is ignored rather
17	 * than sent to the API, which would reject the whole query (S3.6, S3.7).
18	 */
19	export function readListParams(params: URLSearchParams): ListParams {
20	  const zipCode = params.get('zip')?.trim() ?? '';
21	  const state = params.get('state') ?? '';
22	  return {
23	    filter: {
24	      ...NO_FILTER,
25	      city: params.get('city') ?? '',
26	      zipCode: /^\d{5}$/.test(zipCode) ? zipCode : '',
27	      state: STATES.has(state) ? state : '',
28	    },
29	    direction: params.get('sort') === 'oldest' ? SortDirection.ASC : SortDirection.DESC,
30	  };
31	}
32	
33	export function writeListParams({ filter, direction }: ListParams): URLSearchParams {
34	  const params = new URLSearchParams();
35	  const city = filter.city.trim();
36	  const zipCode = filter.zipCode.trim();
37	  if (city) params.set('city', city);
38	  if (zipCode) params.set('zip', zipCode);
39	  if (filter.state) params.set('state', filter.state);
40	  if (direction === SortDirection.ASC) params.set('sort', 'oldest');

1	/** Fields of the create form. `state` is '' until the user picks one. */
2	export interface PropertyFormValues {
3	  street: string;
4	  city: string;
5	  state: string;
6	  zipCode: string;
7	}
8	
9	export type PropertyFormErrors = Partial<Record<keyof PropertyFormValues, string>>;
10	
11	/**
12	 * Mirrors the server rules (SPEC S5.3) so users get instant feedback; the server stays the
13	 * source of truth and its InvalidInputError is shown the same way.
14	 */
15	export function validateProperty(values: PropertyFormValues): PropertyFormErrors {
16	  const errors: PropertyFormErrors = {};
17	  const street = values.street.trim();
18	  const city = values.city.trim();
19	
20	  if (!street) errors.street = 'Street is required.';
21	  else if (street.length > 200) errors.street = 'Street must be at most 200 characters.';
22	
23	  if (!city) errors.city = 'City is required.';
24	  else if (city.length > 100) errors.city = 'City must be at most 100 characters.';
25	
26	  if (!values.state) errors.state = 'State is required.';
27	
28	  if (!/^\d{5}$/.test(values.zipCode)) errors.zipCode = 'Zip code must be exactly 5 digits.';
29	
30	  return errors;
31	}
32
```

1	import { ApolloClient, HttpLink, InMemoryCache } from '@apollo/client';
2	
3	export function createApolloClient(uri: string) {
4	  return new ApolloClient({
5	    link: new HttpLink({ uri }),
6	    cache: new InMemoryCache({
7	      typePolicies: {
8	        // Each result is the complete, unpaginated list for its filter/order (SPEC non-goal:
9	        // pagination), so a new result replaces the old one, including after a delete.
10	        Query: { fields: { properties: { merge: false } } },
11	        // Weather has no id: it is the creation-time snapshot owned by one Property. Merge the
12	        // list's and the detail view's selections instead of letting one overwrite the other.
13	        Weather: { merge: true },
14	      },
15	    }),
16	  });
17	}
18
```

I've sent the full review. Nothing blocks P0: all 42 P0 criteria (S1–S7 and X1–X4) and both P1 items pass, with no FAIL or UNTESTED verdicts. I didn't run anything; every verdict comes from reading the code and its tests, and I didn't modify any files.

The checks you asked for all came back clean:
- **Weatherstack:** it is only reached from the create-property service. `fetch` appears only in `apps/api/src/weatherstack/client.ts`, and the client is built once in `apps/api/src/container.ts`.
- **Failures:** each of the six failure reasons has an integration test. Each one checks for exactly one request, the error returned in `data` rather than `errors[]`, and nothing saved. Validation and the duplicate check run before the Weatherstack call.
- **Lint and generated files:** the only `eslint-disable` lines are in generated files. The codegen drift job in CI would catch hand edits to generated types.
- **Test isolation:** both the API and web test setups use MSW with `onUnhandledRequest: 'error'` and fake keys. e2e points the API at the local stub.

There are 11 non-blocking findings (nine Low, two Info). The ones worth deciding on:
1. **Docs don't match the wiring (X1).** SPEC X1, `CLAUDE.md` and `README.md` say the Weatherstack client is injected through the GraphQL context. In the code it's passed straight into the create-property service, and the context only carries services. That's stricter than the docs, so the wording should change, not the code.
2. **Hand-copied enums.** Two domain types copy GraphQL enums by hand (`client.ts:4-10`, `list-properties.service.ts:12`), so a new schema value wouldn't cause a compile error there.
3. **Delete toast.** "Property deleted" also shows when the server returns `PropertyNotFoundError` (`delete-property-button.tsx:46-48`).
4. **Weak tests:**
   - The web form test that says it "sends nothing" doesn't actually count requests (`create-property-form.test.tsx:48-59`).
   - The concurrent-duplicate test may not always hit the database unique-constraint path (`create-property.int.test.ts:217`).
   - Integration tests will truncate whatever `TEST_DATABASE_URL` points at. The Playwright config has a guard for this; the integration tests don't (`test/test-database-url.ts:2-4`).
5. **Smaller items:**
   - The S7.3 range tests are named S5.5, so a search for "S7.3" finds nothing.
   - A failed refetch leaves a stale list with no notice.
   - The API Docker image includes the whole monorepo and dev dependencies.
   - The key goes over plain HTTP on the free tier; the README already documents this.

The report has the full table with a `file:line` and a named test for every criterion.

</details>

16:| **context7** MCP                    | `.mcp.json`                                           | current docs for Prisma / Yoga / Apollo / codegen / shadcn; their APIs changed across recent majors |
36:| Stop → `stop-check.mjs`                       | typecheck + unit tests, only if source files changed since the last green run; honours `stop_hook_active`         | The model can't declare "done" on red types. Skipping no-op turns keeps Q&A instant, and there's no infinite loop                             |
100:- The docs check (claude-code-guide subagent) showed SessionEnd hooks get a very short time budget, so archiving was changed to re-launch detached. It also showed Bash rule syntax is `Bash(cmd *)`, and that `permissions.ask` might not exist, so `ask` was dropped.
112:**Harness changes:** Everything listed above in Harness was created this session.
126:- Browser walk via Playwright MCP against the stub: duplicate create, create, filters (`"  HILLS "`, `85A68`, `Fo%`), sort, detail, delete, not-found, 390px width. The AI deleted only its own test rows and left the user's dev data unchanged.
131:- The two e2e "hangs" were real bugs, not slowness. (1) Vite bound only `::1`, so Playwright's IPv4 readiness check waited forever. (2) Playwright couldn't exit because `pnpm exec` (pnpm 12 native) starts children in a new process group that Playwright's teardown doesn't kill. Fix: `exec node_modules/.bin/...` plus a graceful SIGTERM. Piping through `tail` also hid progress, which made the first run look stuck.
135:- A web test failed once when run as part of the final `verify`. The AI's first diagnosis (a late async toast) was wrong, and its first fix made the test fail every time. Reading Sonner's source showed the store replays active toasts to a new `<Toaster>`; the fix is `toast.dismiss()` in the test `afterEach`. It was folded into the toasts commit (branch not pushed).
143:**Harness changes:** ESLint now restricts Weatherstack imports to the create-property service; Playwright config and e2e stub added (`apps/web/e2e/`); the Enforcement section above was updated to match. No skill, hook, agent or CLAUDE.md changes.

I found 11 places where a fix sits at the wrong layer; the rest of the diff looks clean at this altitude. The full report, with file:line, root-cause change and cost for each item, went to the caller. Nothing was edited.

The most valuable ones:

1. **List refetches on every visit** (`property-list.tsx:43`): `cache-and-network` is there because creating a property never updates the cache. Adding a cache eviction of `properties` to the create mutation, as delete already does, would let the override go.
2. **Test rebuilds the service wiring by hand** (`create-property.int.test.ts:16-31`): it does this only to set a 100 ms timeout and a silent logger. `createServices` should accept those as options.
3. **Integration test setup repeated in every file**: each one repeats table reset, disconnect and app wiring. A setup file for the integration project would do it once.
4. **e2e shares the integration test database**: that forces a "never run both at once" rule, timestamped streets, and a before/after row comparison. A separate e2e database, reset once before the run, removes all three.
5. **Test database URL and safety check copied three times**: in `playwright.config.ts`, `global-setup.int.ts` and `test-database-url.ts`.
6. **UUID checks pushed up into services**: each by-id service validates the id and delete lowercases it. The repository should handle malformed ids and return the deleted row's id.

Smaller ones:
- **Weather cast and trim in the resolver:** it casts stored weather JSON and trims descriptions on every read; both belong in the repository and the Zod schema.
- **`readEnv` loads `.env` depending on which object it's passed:** loading it at the entrypoints would keep `readEnv` a pure parser.
- **Filter form syncs its draft during render:** it keeps a copy of the applied filter; remounting the form with a `key` is simpler.
- **Stub constants copied into the e2e spec:** the stub can't be imported for them, so `FAILING_ZIP` and the stub URL are duplicated.
- **Zip rule in five places:** this is more a reuse note than an altitude one.

**Clean areas:**
- Resolver-level error messages.
- The Apollo type policies.
- The central `toast.dismiss()` in test setup.
- The Playwright process-group and IPv4 fixes, applied once in config.
- The repository's duplicate-insert and LIKE-escaping handling.
- The Weatherstack client and container.
- ESLint rules, hooks, CI and Docker.

</details>

Or install with `--ignore-scripts` and run generate after the source copy.\n   - Cost: about 20–40 s on every local `docker compose up --build`, plus one needless cross-app rebuild each time.\n\n2. **~/Projects/covertree-properties/.github/workflows/ci.yml:90-96** — the `docker` job runs a plain `docker compose build` with no layer cache.\n   - Every run redoes `apt-get`, `npm i -g pnpm`, `pnpm fetch`, both builds and the nginx stage.\n   - Cheaper: `docker/setup-buildx-action` plus `docker/bake-action` or `build-push-action` with `cache-from/cache-to: type=gha`.\n   - Cost: about 1–3 min per CI run.\n\n3. **ci.yml:80** — the `e2e` job runs `pnpm run build` before e2e, but nothing uses the output.\n   - Playwright starts the API with `tsx src/server.ts` and the web app with the Vite dev server (`apps/web/playwright.config.ts:45,60`).\n   - The build also repeats work other jobs already do: `web build` runs `tsc --noEmit` (the `typecheck` job does that), and both apps are built again in the `docker` job.\n   - Cheaper: drop the step, or make e2e serve the built output (`vite preview` / `node dist/server.js`) so the build is actually tested.\n   - Cost: about 30–60 s on the longest job's critical path.\n\n4. **ci.yml:81** — `playwright install --with-deps chromium` runs uncached on every run.\n   - Cheaper: cache `~/.cache/ms-playwright` keyed on the Playwright version, and only run `install-deps` when the cache hits.\n   - Cost: about 30–60 s per run.\n\n5. **~/Projects/covertree-properties/apps/api/package.json:18, apps/web/package.json:13** — postinstall runs `graphql-codegen` in both apps, even though the generated GraphQL types are committed.\n   - All 5 CI jobs pay for this in the shared setup action. I've sent the efficiency review to the parent agent. I only read code and changed nothing. It lists 14 findings; the ones worth fixing first are in build, CI and the Claude Code hooks.

**Build and CI**
- **Docker rebuilds too much (`Dockerfile:16-18`):** all the source is copied in before `pnpm install`. Any edit re-runs the install and its postinstall steps, and a web-only change also rebuilds the API. That's roughly 20–40 s on every local rebuild.
- **The CI `docker` job has no layer cache (`ci.yml:90-96`):** every run builds from scratch, costing about 1–3 minutes.
- **The `e2e` job runs a build nothing uses (`ci.yml:80`):** Playwright runs the apps from source, so the build step just adds about 30–60 s to the longest job.
- **Other CI waste:** Playwright browsers are downloaded on every run (about 30–60 s). GraphQL codegen runs on install in all 5 jobs even though its output is committed.
- **Deployed web and API images:** the web server has gzip off, so bundles go out about 3–4x larger. The API image carries the whole workspace's dependencies, probably several hundred MB.

**Hooks**
- **Format/lint hook:** it calls prettier and eslint through `pnpm exec`, which costs about 0.6–1.2 s of pnpm startup on every edit. It already checks the tools are installed locally, so it can call them directly.
- **Stop hook:** it runs typecheck and unit tests one after the other across every package. Running them at the same time, and only for changed packages, saves several seconds per stop.
- **Codegen hook:** it starts a second Node process on every edit only to exit. Folding it into the format hook saves about 50 ms per edit.

**App and tests** (smaller)
- After creating a property, the detail page makes a second request. The create mutation only returns the new property's `id`, so the page has nothing to show from the cache.
- The property list refetches every time you come back to it from a detail page.
- The filter tests re-seed 5 rows before each of 6 tests, though only one test changes the data.
- The session-archiving script rebuilds its redaction rules for every line of a transcript. It runs in the background, so this is low priority.

**Clean:** the API layer (the duplicate check before insert deliberately avoids an unneeded weather API call), docker-compose, the integration test setup, and the Weatherstack client.

The full report has file:line references, the cheaper alternative for each item, and cost estimates.

</details>

   121	
   122	/**
   123	 * Render transcript entries as Markdown blocks: user prompts, assistant text, tool calls with
   124	 * inputs, truncated tool results. Meta entries (attachments, mode, cost, snapshots) are dropped.
   125	 */
   126	export function renderEntries(entries) {
   127	  const blocks = [];
   128	  const toolNames = new Map();
   129	  let lastRole = null;
   130	
   131	  for (const e of entries) {
   132	    if (e.type === 'system' && e.subtype === 'compact_boundary') {
   133	      blocks.push('---\n\n_Context compacted — earlier turns summarized._');
   134	      lastRole = null;
   135	      continue;
   136	    }
   137	    if (e.type !== 'user' && e.type !== 'assistant') continue;
   138	    if (e.isMeta || e.isCompactSummary) continue;
   139	    const content = e.message?.content;
   140	
   141	    if (e.type === 'user') {
   142	      if (typeof content === 'string') {
   143	        const md = renderUserString(content);
   144	        if (md) {
   145	          blocks.push(md.startsWith('####') ? `${md}  \n<sub>${time(e.timestamp)} UTC</sub>` : md);
   146	          lastRole = 'user';
   147	        }
   148	        continue;
   149	      }
   150	      for (const c of content ?? []) {
   151	        if (c.type === 'tool_result') {
   152	          const name = toolNames.get(c.tool_use_id) ?? 'tool';
   153	          const text = stripNoise(resultText(c.content));
   154	          const human = userVoice(text);
   155	          if (human) {
   156	            blocks.push(
   157	              `#### 🧑 User ${human.label} (${name})  \n<sub>${time(e.timestamp)} UTC</sub>\n\n${human.text}`,
   158	            );
   159	            lastRole = 'user';
   160	            continue;
   161	          }
   162	          const lines = text.split('\n').length;
   163	          blocks.push(
   164	            `<details><summary>${c.is_error ? '❌ ' : ''}${name} result (${lines} line${lines === 1 ? '' : 's'})</summary>\n\n${fence(truncate(text))}\n\n</details>`,
   165	          );
   166	        } else if (c.type === 'text') {
   167	          const text = stripNoise(c.text);
   168	          if (text) {
   169	            blocks.push(`#### 🧑 User\n\n${text}`);
   170	            lastRole = 'user';
   171	          }
   172	        } else if (c.type === 'image') {
   173	          blocks.push('_[image attached]_');
   174	        }
   175	      }
   176	      continue;
   177	    }
   178	
   179	    for (const c of content ?? []) {
   180	      if (c.type === 'text' && c.text.trim()) {
   181	        if (lastRole !== 'assistant') blocks.push('#### 🤖 Claude');
   182	        blocks.push(c.text.trim());
   183	        lastRole = 'assistant';
   184	      } else if (c.type === 'tool_use') {
   185	        if (lastRole !== 'assistant') blocks.push('#### 🤖 Claude');
   186	        toolNames.set(c.id, c.name);
   187	        blocks.push(renderToolUse(c));
   188	        lastRole = 'assistant';
   189	      }
   190	    }
   191	  }
   192	  return blocks;
   193	}
   194	
   195	/** Fallback title: the first real prompt, shell command or slash command, shortened. */
   196	function firstPrompt(entries) {
   197	  for (const e of entries) {
   198	    if (e.type !== 'user' || e.isMeta || typeof e.message?.content !== 'string') continue;
   199	    const raw = e.message.content;
   200	    const text = (
   201	      tag(raw, 'command-name') ??
   202	      tag(raw, 'bash-input')?.replace(/^/, '$ ') ??
   203	      stripNoise(raw)
   204	    )
   205	      .replace(/(?:\\n|\s)+/g, ' ')
   206	      .trim();
   207	    if (text && !/^<(bash-std|local-command)/.test(raw))
   208	      return text.length > 60 ? `${text.slice(0, 57)}…` : text;
   209	  }
   210	  return null;
   211	}
   212	
   213	/** Summary metadata used for the session header and the index. */
   214	export function summarize(entries) {
   215	  const stamps = entries
   216	    .map((e) => e.timestamp)
   217	    .filter(Boolean)
   218	    .sort();
   219	  const title = entries.findLast((e) => e.type === 'ai-title')?.aiTitle ?? firstPrompt(entries);
   220	  const models = new Set(
   221	    entries
   222	      .filter(
   223	        (e) => e.type === 'assistant' && e.message?.model && e.message.model !== '<synthetic>',
   224	      )
   225	      .map((e) => e.message.model),
   226	  );
   227	  let prompts = 0;
   228	  let toolCalls = 0;
   229	  for (const e of entries) {
   230	    if (e.isMeta) continue;
   231	    const c = e.message?.content;
   232	    if (
   233	      e.type === 'user' &&
   234	      typeof c === 'string' &&
   235	      !/<(bash-stdout|local-command-stdout)>/.test(c)
   236	    )
   237	      prompts++;
   238	    if (
   239	      e.type === 'user' &&
   240	      Array.isArray(c) &&
   241	      c.some(
   242	        (b) =>
   243	          (b.type === 'text' && stripNoise(b.text)) ||
   244	          (b.type === 'tool_result' && userVoice(stripNoise(resultText(b.content)))),
   245	      )
   246	    )
   247	      prompts++;
   248	    if (e.type === 'assistant' && Array.isArray(c))
   249	      toolCalls += c.filter((b) => b.type === 'tool_use').length;
   250	  }
   251	  return {
   252	    sessionId: entries.find((e) => e.sessionId)?.sessionId ?? null,
   253	    started: stamps[0] ?? null,
   254	    ended: stamps.at(-1) ?? null,
   255	    title,
   256	    models: [...models],
   257	    prompts,
   258	    toolCalls,
   259	  };
   260	}
   261	
   262	export function renderSession({ entries, subagents = [] }) {
   263	  const s = summarize(entries);
   264	  const out = [
   265	    `# ${s.title ?? 'Claude Code session'}`,
   266	    '',
   267	    `- **Session:** \`${s.sessionId}\``,
   268	    `- **Started:** ${s.started ?? 'unknown'}`,
   269	    `- **Ended:** ${s.ended ?? 'unknown'}`,
   270	    `- **Models:** ${s.models.join(', ') || 'n/a'}`,
   271	    `- **Prompts:** ${s.prompts} · **Tool calls:** ${s.toolCalls} · **Subagents:** ${subagents.length}`,
   272	    '',
   273	    '> Rendered by `scripts/archive-session.mjs`. Tool outputs are truncated and sensitive values',
   274	    '> redacted; the redacted raw transcript is in `raw/`.',
   275	    '',
   276	    ...renderEntries(entries).flatMap((b) => [b, '']),
   277	  ];
   278	  if (subagents.length) {
   279	    out.push('---', '', '## Subagents', '');
   280	    for (const a of subagents) {
   281	      const label =
   282	        [a.meta?.agentType, a.meta?.description].filter(Boolean).join(' — ') || a.agentId;
   283	      out.push(
   284	        `<details><summary>🧩 ${label} (<code>${a.agentId}</code>)</summary>`,
   285	        '',
   286	        ...renderEntries(a.entries).flatMap((b) => [b, '']),
   287	        '</details>',
   288	        '',
   289	      );
   290	    }
   291	  }
   292	  return out.join('\n');
   293	}

  "message": "I found 16 simplifications I'm confident about, 10 in the app and 6 in tests and config. Nothing was edited. All paths are under ~/Projects/covertree-properties.\n\n**Web app (apps/web/src)**\n\n1. **features/properties/property-filters.tsx:41-51**: state that could be derived from props.\n   - Now: the component keeps a copy of `applied` in `prevApplied`, compares it with `sameFilter` (lines 27-28), and calls `setDraft` and `setZipError` during render to stay in sync.\n   - Simpler: in property-list.tsx:64, render `<PropertyFilters key={writeListParams({filter, direction: SortDirection.DESC}).toString()} applied={filter} … />`. A remount then resets both `draft` and `zipError`, so `prevApplied`, `sameFilter` and the block at lines 47-51 can go.\n   - Cost: one extra state variable, a hand-written equality function that must be updated whenever a field is added, and the setState-during-render pattern that readers find hard to follow.\n\n2. **features/properties/property-list.tsx:66-112**: a four-level nested ternary picks one of five UI states (error, loading, filtered-empty, empty, list).\n   - Simpler: move it into a `ListBody` component that uses early returns, the same way `PropertyDetails` (property-details.tsx:24-67) already does.\n   - Cost: the conditions depend on their order and are hard to scan. `properties?.length === 0` is repeated, and `properties?.map` has to stay optional-chained even though `properties` is known to be defined by then.\n\n3. **property-list.tsx:66-80 and property-details.tsx:24-41**: the \"Could not load … / Check that the API is running / Retry\" Alert is copy-pasted, differing only in the title.\n   - Simpler: a shared `<LoadError title onRetry />`.\n   - Cost: about 15 duplicated lines of JSX, and a copy or styling change has to be made twice.\n\n4. **property-list.tsx:26-29 and property-details.tsx:15-18**: the same `Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-US', {dateStyle:'medium', timeStyle:'short'})` is defined twice.\n   - Simpler: export one formatter from a shared module.\n   - Cost: the two dates can drift apart in format.\n\n5. **`Object.values(USState)` defined three times**: create-property-form.tsx:19, property-filters.tsx:8, and as a Set in list-search-params.ts:13. I've sent the full simplification review to the main agent. It lists 16 findings I'm confident about, 10 in the app code and 6 in tests and config. Nothing was edited.

The ones worth doing first:

- **Filter form copies URL state:** `property-filters.tsx:41-51` keeps a copy of the applied filters and resyncs it during render. Giving the component a `key` built from the applied filter values makes it reset on its own, so that state and its equality helper can go.
- **Nested ternary in the list:** `property-list.tsx:66-112` picks between five screen states with a four-level ternary. A small component with early returns would read like `property-details.tsx` already does.
- **Copy-pasted UI and constants:**
  - The "could not load / Retry" alert is duplicated in the list and detail views.
  - So is the date formatter.
  - The US state list is defined three times and its dropdown options twice.
  - The 5-digit zip check is written in five places across web and API.
- **Leftovers:**
  - An object spread in `list-search-params.ts:24` whose fields are all overwritten.
  - An unused `export` on `FAILING_ZIP` in the e2e stub.
  - `routes/home.tsx`, which only renders `<PropertyList />`.
  - An input type in the create-property service that repeats the repository's `Address` type field for field.
- **Settings that repeat defaults:**
  - Vite's port 5173 in `vite.config.ts`.
  - The API port and Weatherstack URL in `docker-compose.yml`, which match the env defaults.
  - Most of the CI `env:` block. I worked this last one out from reading the configs, not by running CI; only `DATABASE_URL` looks needed there.

In the tests, `app.test.ts` rebuilds a request helper that already exists, and the web tests define the same list-row fixture twice.

These areas came back clean: the API resolvers, repository, services and Weatherstack client; the hook scripts; the session-archiving scripts; the Dockerfile, nginx and eslint config.

</details>
